# AER Layer 4 review pack, re-issue: reconciled to the Layer 4 Inbox, 8 November 2026

Prepared for the Platform Admin (directive 8 Nov 2026: reconcile the 184-item verified review pack against the live Layer 4 Inbox; resolve the difference between review-pack readiness and Inbox readiness, including Approve except and stale items; present complete evidence-backed packets through the existing Layer 4 UI). Status: read-only preparation. No Layer 4 item was approved, rejected or changed, no setting or flag was touched, and nothing was written to production. This file replaces `aer-review-pack-20261107.md`; the Controller places it in the admin repository.

**Nothing here is a decision.** Human Platform Admin decisions are mandatory for every item. The attention levels and every "suggestion" below only say where a person might spend time; they approve, reject and admit nothing. No blanket approval is proposed.

Evidence base: live Supabase project `fxcwkweaxjtknorudmwp`, read only, on 8 Nov 2026 at about 11:30 AEDT (review items, readiness table, Inbox settlement, proposed rows, locks, supersession history, stored copies and the live completeness gate). Rows carried over from the 7 Nov pack were re-verified: all 184 are still waiting, none has been decided, the gates still pass (180) or the course is stale (4).

## 1. Answer in brief

* The review pack and the Inbox counted different things. The pack's 184 were the items the **readiness table** called `judgement_ready`. The Inbox "Ready to decide" counts the items a person can decide **now**, from the **settlement** classification: the check passes (Approve all rows or Reject) or it names the rows to leave out (**Approve except**), and the read is not **stale**. Per the platform's own rule (a person can usefully decide an item whose evidence is complete, or an Approve except item whose exception rows are listed) the Inbox definition is the right one, and the scheduling counter that used the readiness table was wrong in three ways (section 3).
* Today the Inbox shows **245** ready to decide. This pack lists all 245 (section 10), plus the **26 stale** reads (section 10.5). It replaces the 7 Nov tables, which showed 180 of the 245 and 4 of the 26.
* 245 = **187** complete and not locked + **20** complete but with a lock on the course page address + **25** Approve except (not locked) + **13** Approve except with a lock. The 33 locked items are listed separately as **held unless individually authorised** (section 10.6).
* **10** of the Approve except matrix runs name every row to leave out, so Approve except would admit nothing (section 10.4). They are flagged for judgement, not hidden.
* The code change (migration `20261108040000`, not applied; the Controller deploys) makes one definition (`security.aer_review_ready_v1`, which reads the Inbox's settlement) the source for the Inbox and for the scheduling counts, and classifies new items at once (section 4).

The counter's 203 is 199 non-duplicate ready items plus the 4 stale reads it wrongly counted as ready; the Inbox's 245 is the 199 plus the 8 matrix runs the counter called duplicates plus the 38 Approve except items.

**The difference between the 7 Nov Inbox figure (226) and today's 245:** 226 was the pack's 180 ready items plus 46 Approve except items (24 course reads and 22 matrix runs). Today it is the same 180 plus 27 items that became ready since (18 reads recreated from stored records by the `aer-replay` job, 1 older Torrens read that passed its gate, and 8 Australian National University matrix runs whose gate now passes) plus 38 Approve except items (24 course reads, 14 matrix runs: the 8 ANU runs moved from "Approve except" to "complete"). The pack's 4 stale items are unchanged.

## 3. Where "ready" is defined, and which definition is right

| Place | Definition | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| `security.aer_settlement_v1` (stored records only), read by `layer4_aer_university_summary_v1`, `layer4_aer_queue_v1`, `public.admin_layer4_aer_queue` and `src/Layer4Inbox.jsx` ("Ready to decide", the AER summary line, the ready page) | `ready` when the item is gated and not stale and not locked on entry requirements and its route is `review`, `review_except` or `matrix_except`; `stale` when the course is inactive or its bound page is not the page read; `recoverable` when not yet gated or an untried corrected read exists; otherwise `needs_input`. Matrix runs and course reads alike, one row per waiting item | What a person sees and decides. **This is the right definition.** |
| `pipeline.layer4_item_readiness.readiness` (job `layer4-readiness`, runs at :22 and :52) | `judgement_ready` when the item has no blocker; any incomplete gate (including Approve except) is `technical_blocked` | The review pack of 7 Nov, `verification_measures`, the readiness reports |
| `security.work_review_ready_v1('aer')` and `security.aer_provider_load_v1` (the scheduling room, 600 limit, `wc.aer_fair_on`) | `judgement_ready` only, one item per course (or per university for matrix runs), unclassified counted ready | How many more reads may be started |
| `security.aer_fair_projection_v1` | routes `review`, `review_except`, `matrix_except` are reviewer-ready, one per entity and kind | A projection only (not changed; it already states the intended rule, and shares the one-per-university matrix assumption) |

The scheduling counter was wrong against the platform's own rule in three ways, all shown by live data:

1. **Approve except counted as held.** The 24 course reads and 14 matrix runs need a person to leave out listed rows. A repair cannot settle them, so "technically held" is wrong. All 38 list their exception rows (not a requirement, a domestic route stored for all, or rows sharing one identity).
2. **Matrix runs called duplicates.** 24 ANU and Melbourne matrix runs have 24 different source pages and 24 different run ids. Each is its own decision. The rule "one per university and kind" is only right for a course (one course, one read).
3. **Stale reads counted as ready.** The four packed stale reads (Lincoln University 3, Victoria University of Wellington 1) are bound to library or research-archive records, so the read no longer describes the course's page. A person can only reject them.

Code change (migration `20261108040000_cf247_aer_review_ready_single_definition.sql`, branch `cf247-aer-review-ready`, not applied): `security.aer_review_ready_v1` states the definition once (it reads the Inbox's own `aer_settlement_v1`, which is not changed) and adds the class, the exception rows listed, the URL-lock flag and the true-duplicate rule. `work_review_ready_v1` and `aer_provider_load_v1` now read it, so the counter equals the Inbox's "Ready to decide" except for true duplicates (none live). The 600 limit and all settings are unchanged. Consumer API: untouched.

Not decided by the change, and listed in section 9: whether a lock on the course page address holds a first read (it is reported, not applied), and whether an Approve except item that must leave out every row counts as ready.

## 4. Readiness of newly replayed items

* `aer-replay` (hourly at :33) creates a new waiting item (`course_entry_first_read`) from stored records. The readiness table is filled by job `layer4-readiness` (runs at :22 and :52; never-gated items first, at most `layer4.gate_batch` gates a run), so a new item has no readiness row, and the Inbox shows it as "not yet checked" (settlement `recoverable`), for up to 19 minutes (replayed at :33, gated at :52).
* Live check of the 18 replayed items (13 RMIT): every one now has a readiness row, `judgement_ready`, gate complete (E13: no row cut, no row about another programme, nothing dropped). The first gate came at the next :52 run, 19 minutes after creation (for example created 13:33 UTC, gated 13:52 UTC). Running the live gate again for two RMIT items (`70461654` Master of Engineering (Electrical and Electronic Engineering), `85cc1aac` Master of Engineering (Robotics and Mechatronics Engineering)) returned complete, no reasons, no flagged row. All 18 are ready to decide in the Inbox and are in section 10.
* Change: `security.layer4_readiness_item_v1` classifies one waiting item exactly as the run did (the run now calls it, with the same result for every item in the fixture: 605 items classified identically before and after), and `security.layer4_readiness_items_v1(uuid[])` classifies a list now. **The replay should call, once, after it has created its new items: `perform security.layer4_readiness_items_v1(v_new_item_ids);` (a `uuid[]` of the `new_item_id` values; wrap it so a failure does not stop the replay).** It evaluates the gate for at most `layer4.gate_batch` items, honours `layer4.readiness_on`, records only, and decides nothing.

## 6. What the reviewer sees today, and how to reach it

Unchanged from section 2 and 3 of the 7 Nov pack except as follows. Layer 4 Review > Inbox > University view > the university > kind pill **Entry requirements (AER)** > **Ready to decide** (the default). That list now matches this pack: it holds the complete reads, the Approve except reads and the locked reads together (oldest first, 25 a page). A route label on each item says Approve all rows or Approve except. **Stale** items are not in the "Ready to decide" list; use **All waiting** (the summary line shows the stale count). Decisions stay one item at a time, Platform Admin only, each with a reason: Approve all rows, Approve except (tick the rows to leave out), or Reject the read. No bulk path exists for AER items.

After each authorised decision the verification below is run before the next item (Platform Admin directive): the decision is recorded (`pipeline.layer4_decisions`), the rows are admitted (`catalogue.course_entry_requirements` or `catalogue.provider_entry_matrix` status active), the admission check passes (`pipeline.layer4_admission_checks`), and the existing Website and Zoho publication is confirmed. All 221 course reads in the ready list are for courses that are not yet published and hold no active entry rows (live check 8 Nov), so approval is a first admission with nothing replaced and puts nothing on the website until the course is published; that is the separate, authorised publication step. Matrix runs add rows to the provider entry matrix. Nothing is left between stages: an approved item is followed until admitted and (where its course is published) projected.

## 7. Decision patterns (suggestions)

* **Complete and unambiguous (Routine).** Every check passes, every row has a quote, page and stored copy, confidence is 0.90 or higher, no quote is at the cap, no lock, no dropped requirement. Suggestion: read the quotes, then decide.
* **Evidence present, one thing to glance at (Check).** A free-text grade with no scale, a quote cut at the 300-character cap, a requirement the reader dropped but another row covers, or a row with confidence below 0.90. Suggestion: check the flagged row against the stored copy.
* **Approve except (Check or Judgement).** The gate names the rows to leave out and the card lists them (marked **LEAVE OUT** in section 10). Suggestion: tick only the named rows. Where the named row is a domestic-only route stored for all audiences (Amtech Institute `6a992316`), or where every row of a matrix run is named (section 10.4), a person judges.
* **Locked (Ruling first).** The course's page address is locked by hand and the read used that same page. The packet recommends rejecting the read; the lock is on the address fields, not on entry requirements, and the approval code does not check locks. Suggestion: one Platform Admin ruling on whether such a lock holds a first read, then the 33 follow it. Until then they are held unless individually authorised (section 10.6).
* **Stale (Judgement).** The read is of a page the course is no longer bound to. Suggestion: reject the read, and ask the owner of coverage to correct the binding (section 10.5).
* **Judgement.** The RMIT override rows, the citizenship limit (Victoria University of Wellington, Doctor of Health), the Newcastle empty run, and the matrix runs where Approve except would admit nothing.

## 10. Item tables

Ref is the first 8 characters of the review item id, as shown on the card ("Ref"). Each row of a read is labelled R1, R2 and so on in both the requirement and the quote columns. Quotes are exactly as stored; "[stored quote is at the 300-character cap; may be cut]" marks a quote stored at 300 characters. "free text, no scale" marks a grade row with no scale. Rows from the 7 Nov pack keep their original check wording ("live gate, 7 Nov"); re-verified 8 Nov. Attention is a suggestion.

### 10.2 Ready to decide: matrix runs, evidence complete

Provider entry matrix runs whose completeness check (D-R3) passes. Approve adds the run's rows to the provider entry matrix (website provider matrix); nothing is replaced unless the row is already active (none are). Each run is one source page, so a university can have several runs (ANU has 22 waiting runs from 22 pages).

### 10.5 Stale reads (not in Ready to decide; under All waiting)

A read is stale when the course is inactive or bound to a page that is not the page the read came from. The suggestion for every one is the same: reject the read (it no longer describes the course's bound page) and have the binding corrected by the owner of coverage; a corrected binding makes a new first read, not a correction. The first five are the stale items whose evidence was otherwise complete or Approve except (the four of the 7 Nov pack and `3ed15431`); the other 21 are also held for the reason in the Route column.

## 11. Method and limits

* Read only. Queries on the live project covered the review items, readiness table, Inbox settlement (`security.aer_settlement_v1`), proposed rows, locks, supersession history, stored-copy records and the live gate (run for two items only). Live definitions were read with `pg_get_functiondef` and compared by md5 with the checked-in definitions; they match.
* Counts move as the hourly jobs run (`aer-replay` at :33, `layer4-readiness` at :22 and :52). The figures here are one consistent read on 8 Nov 2026 at about 11:30 AEDT.
* "Scores in quote" compares a stored score with the numbers in its own quote; it does not read the stored page. Whether a quote is truly on the page is the gate's own test.
* The 300-character cap is inferred from stored quotes of exactly that length; its source was not traced.
* Full item ids are in `pipeline.layer4_review_items`; the 8-character Ref is the card's own identifier and is unique across the items listed.
